TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

- Metric: The task asks for the "oldest released song," so we need to use the 'release_date' field to determine the oldest song. The 'release_date' field is confirmed to exist in the show_song API response.
- Ambiguities: The task does not specify whether to consider only songs directly in the song library or also songs inside albums and playlists. To be comprehensive, we consider all three containers and their songs.
- API quirks: The song library API returns songs with 'added_at' but not 'release_date', so we must call show_song for each song to get the 'release_date'. Albums have 'release_date' but we need songs inside albums, so we must get song IDs from albums and then get each song's release_date. Playlists have 'created_at' but not release_date, so we must get song IDs from playlists and then get each song's release_date.

6. [spotify] FOR EACH song_id in the combined set: Use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'release_date' and 'title'. [field: 'release_date']
7. [generic] Find the song with the oldest 'release_date' among all fetched songs.
8. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=<title_of_oldest_released_song>)

ReAct step 5 / attempt 1
# Extract song_ids from song library
song_ids_from_songs = {song['song_id'] for song in song_library}

# Extract song_ids from albums
song_ids_from_albums = set()
for album in album_library:
    song_ids_from_albums.update(album.get('song_ids', []))

# Extract song_ids from playlists
song_ids_from_playlists = set()
for playlist in playlist_library:
    song_ids_from_playlists.update(playlist.get('song_ids', []))

# Combine all song_ids into a unique set
all_song_ids = song_ids_from_songs.union(song_ids_from_albums).union(song_ids_from_playlists)

print(f"Total unique song IDs combined: {len(all_song_ids)}")
print("Sample combined song IDs:", list(all_song_ids)[:10])

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
from datetime import datetime

# Parse release_date and find the oldest song
oldest_song = None
oldest_date = None

for song in songs_details:
    release_date_str = song.get('release_date')
    if release_date_str:
        try:
            release_date = datetime.fromisoformat(release_date_str)
            if oldest_date is None or release_date < oldest_date:
                oldest_date = release_date
                oldest_song = song
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error parsing date for song {song.get('song_id')}: {e}")

if oldest_song:
    print(f"Oldest song title: {oldest_song.get('title')}, release date: {oldest_date.isoformat()}")
else:
    print("No valid release dates found.")

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

go_to_sleep_alarm = None
other_alarms_to_disable = []

for alarm in alarms:
    label = alarm.get('label', '').lower()
    if 'go-to-sleep' in label or 'go to sleep' in label:
        go_to_sleep_alarm = alarm
    else:
        if alarm.get('enabled', False):
            other_alarms_to_disable.append(alarm)

if go_to_sleep_alarm:
    old_time_str = go_to_sleep_alarm['time']
    old_time = datetime.strptime(old_time_str, '%H:%M')
    new_time = (old_time + timedelta(hours=1)).time()
    new_time_str = new_time.strftime('%H:%M')
else:
    new_time_str = None

print("Go-to-sleep alarm:", go_to_sleep_alarm)
print("New time for go-to-sleep alarm:", new_time_str)
print("Other alarms to disable:", other_alarms_to_disable)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

import re
from datetime import datetime

wake_up_alarm = None
for alarm in alarms:
    label = alarm.get('label', '').lower()
    if re.search(r'wake[- ]?up', label):
        wake_up_alarm = alarm
        break

if not wake_up_alarm:
    enabled_alarms = [a for a in alarms if a.get('enabled')]
    if enabled_alarms:
        wake_up_alarm = sorted(enabled_alarms, key=lambda a: datetime.strptime(a['time'], '%H:%M'))[0]

print("Wake-up alarm identified:", wake_up_alarm)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

filtered_songs = []
current_year = datetime.now().year
valid_years = {current_year, current_year - 1}

for song in detailed_songs:
    genre = song.get('genre', '').lower()
    release_date_str = song.get('release_date', '')
    if genre == 'r&b' and release_date_str:
        try:
            release_year = datetime.fromisoformat(release_date_str).year
            if release_year in valid_years:
                filtered_songs.append(song)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error parsing date for song {song.get('song_id')}: {e}")

print("Filtered R&B songs released this or last year:", filtered_songs)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

12:00:00Pre-injecting API docs for: ['spotify', 'file_system']
📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We iterate all songs in the user's song library and all albums in the user's album library because the task requires cleaning up these libraries. We also fetch all liked songs and liked albums to determine which to keep. For albums, we check if all songs in the album are downloaded to consider the album downloaded.
- Metric: The key fields are 'song_id' for songs, 'album_id' for albums, and 'song_ids' for songs in an album. We use 'show_song_library' for songs in library, 'show_liked_songs' for liked songs, 'show_downloaded_songs' for downloaded songs, 'show_album_library' for albums in library, 'show_liked_albums' for liked albums, and 'show_album' to get songs in an album. The downloaded status of an album is determined by checking if all its songs are downloaded.
- Ambiguities: The task states "keep only those songs and albums in my song and album library, respectively, that I have liked and downloaded." For albums, "downloaded" means all songs in the album are downloaded. We interpret "keep" as retaining only those liked and downloaded songs/albums and removing others from both the library and downloads.
- API quirks: The 'show_album' API returns the songs in the album under 'songs' with each song having an 'id' field. The 'show_downloaded_songs' API returns downloaded songs with 'song_id'. Removing songs/albums from library and downloads requires separate API calls.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for all subsequent Spotify API calls.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_songs', token) to get all songs the user has liked. [field: 'song_id']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_downloaded_songs', token) to get all songs the user has downloaded. [field: 'song_id']
5. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
6. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_albums', token) to get all albums the user has liked. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
7. [spotify] For each album from step 5, call call_api('spotify', 'show_album', token, album_id=album['album_id']) to get the list of songs in the album. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
8. [generic] Determine which songs to keep: intersection of liked songs (step 3) and downloaded songs (step 4). Determine which songs to remove: songs in library (step 2) but not in keep set.
9. [generic] Determine which albums to keep: albums that are liked (step 6) and have all their songs downloaded (from step 7 and step 4). Determine which albums to remove: albums in library (step 5) but not in keep set.
10. [spotify] For each song to remove (from step 8), call call_api('spotify', 'remove_song_from_library', token, song_id=song_id) and call_api('spotify', 'remove_downloaded_song', token, song_id=song_id) to remove it from library and downloads.
11. [spotify] For each album to remove (from step 9), call call_api('spotify', 'remove_album_from_library', token, album_id=album_id) to remove it from the album library. (No explicit API to remove downloaded albums, so removing songs individually suffices.)
12. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None) to indicate task completion.

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

ReAct step 3 / attempt 1
token = login('simple_note')
movie_notes_contents = []
for note in notes_metadata:
    # Focus on notes likely to contain movie titles by title or tags
    if 'movie' in note['title'].lower() or 'leisure' in (note.get('tags') or []):
        full_note = call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', token, note_id=note['note_id'])
        movie_notes_contents.append(full_note)
        print(f"Note title: {full_note['title']}")
        print(f"Content preview: {full_note['content'][:100]}")  # print first 100 chars for brevity
print(f"Retrieved {len(movie_notes_contents)} movie-related notes.")

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
# Extract movie titles from the "Movie Recommendations" note content
movie_titles = []

for note in movie_notes_contents:
    content = note.get('content', '')
    lines = content.splitlines()
    # Heuristic: movie titles are lines that are not empty and not starting with '-'
    for line in lines:
        line = line.strip()
        if line and not line.startswith('-') and not line.startswith('#'):
            # Avoid lines that look like section headers or metadata
            if not any(keyword in line.lower() for keyword in ['director', 'genre', 'authors', 'quotes', 'book']):
                movie_titles.append(line)

# Remove duplicates and join into a comma-separated string
unique_titles = sorted(set(movie_titles))
movie_titles_string = ', '.join(unique_titles)
print("Extracted movie titles:", movie_titles_string)

def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
content = movie_note.get('content', '')
lines = content.splitlines()

movie_titles = []
for line in lines:
    line = line.strip()
    if line and not line.startswith('#') and not line.startswith('-') and not line.lower().startswith('director') and not line.lower().startswith('genre'):
        # This line is likely a movie title
        movie_titles.append(line)

# Remove duplicates while preserving order
seen = set()
unique_titles = []
for title in movie_titles:
    if title not in seen:
        seen.add(title)
        unique_titles.append(title)

movie_titles_str = ', '.join(unique_titles)
print(movie_titles_str)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

from datetime import datetime, timedelta

token = login('phone')
current_dt = call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token)
date_str = current_dt.get('date')  # e.g. 'Thursday, May 18, 2023'

# Parse the date string with the correct format
today_date = datetime.strptime(date_str, '%A, %B %d, %Y')
yesterday_date = today_date - timedelta(days=1)

today_str = today_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')
yesterday_str = yesterday_date.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's Spotify song library to find those that are not liked by the user.
- Metric: The "liked" status is determined by comparing song IDs from the song library and liked songs. The rating field is 'rating' confirmed by show_song_reviews API.
- Ambiguities: The task requires rating songs not liked with 1 star and if already rated higher, decrease to 1 star. We interpret "already rated it higher" as any rating > 1.
- API quirks: show_song_reviews returns reviews with 'song_review_id' as the review ID and includes 'rating' and 'user' with 'email'. review_song creates a review, update_song_review updates an existing review.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get ALL songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id']
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_liked_songs', token) to get ALL songs the user has liked. [field: 'song_id']
4. [generic] Compute the set difference of song_ids from the song library minus liked songs to get songs not liked.
5. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'show_profile', token) to get the user's email (to filter reviews by user).
6. [spotify] FOR EACH song_id in the not liked songs:
   - Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_reviews', token, song_id=song_id, user_email=user_email) to get the user's review for that song.
   - If a review exists and rating > 1, use call_api('spotify', 'update_song_review', token, review_id=song_review_id, rating=1) to update the rating to 1.
   - If no review exists, use call_api('spotify', 'review_song', token, song_id=song_id, rating=1) to create a 1-star review.
7. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

- API quirks: 
  - show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'song_ids' list.
  - show_playlist returns detailed playlist info including 'songs' list with each song having 'id' and 'artist_ids'.
  - show_song returns 'genre' and 'artists' (with 'id' and 'name').
  - follow_artist requires 'artist_id' and access_token.
  - We must avoid following artists already followed, so we will fetch the list of currently followed artists via show_following_artists.

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
  - Scope: Iterate all playlists in the user's playlist library, then iterate all songs in each playlist to find all artists of those songs.
  - Metric: Filter songs by the genre field 'genre' from the show_song API to identify indie-genre songs.
  - Ambiguities: The task specifies "indie-genre songs" so we must check each song's genre field for "indie" or related indie subgenres. We assume a substring match on the genre string for "indie".
  - API quirks: 
    • show_playlist_library returns playlists with a 'song_ids' list.
    • show_playlist returns detailed songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids'.
    • show_song returns 'genre' as a string and 'artists' as a list of artist objects with 'id'.
    • follow_artist requires artist_id and access_token.
    • show_following_artists can be used to avoid following artists already followed.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for all subsequent calls.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get ALL playlists in the user's library. [field: 'song_ids']
3. [spotify] For EACH playlist from step 2, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed song info including song IDs and artist IDs. [field: 'songs' with 'id' and 'artist_ids']
4. [spotify] Collect all unique song IDs from all playlists' songs. For EACH unique song ID, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including the 'genre' field. [field: 'genre']
5. [generic] Filter the songs from step 4 to keep only those where the 'genre' string contains "indie" (case-insensitive substring match).
6. [generic] Collect all unique artist IDs from the filtered indie songs' 'artists' list.
7. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_following_artists', token) to get the list of artists the user is already following. [field: 'artist_id']
8. [generic] Filter the artist IDs from step 6 to exclude those already followed from step 7.
9. [spotify] FOR EACH artist ID from step 8, use call_api('spotify', 'follow_artist', token, artist_id=artist_id) to follow the artist.
10. [supervisor] apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None) to indicate task completion.

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

Execution successful.

all_song_ids = set()
for playlist in detailed_playlists:
    for song in playlist.get('songs', []):
        all_song_ids.add(song['id'])

songs_details = []
for song_id in all_song_ids:

    songs_details.append(song_detail)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify
REASONING:
- Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's Spotify song library and all songs in all playlists in the user's playlist library to remove those released before 2021.
- Metric: The release date of songs is given by the 'release_date' field in the show_song API, confirmed by get_api_details for show_song.
- Ambiguities: The task specifies "songs released before 2021," so we interpret this as any song with a release_date earlier than 2021-01-01.
- API quirks: The show_song_library API returns songs with an 'added_at' field but not 'release_date', so we must call show_song for each song to get the 'release_date'. The show_playlist API returns songs with 'id' but no release_date, so again we must call show_song for each song in playlists to get release_date before deciding removal.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'song_id']
3. [spotify] For each song from step 2, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song['song_id']) to get the song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
4. [spotify] Remove from the song library all songs with 'release_date' before '2021-01-01' using remove_song_from_library(song_id=song['song_id'], access_token=token).
5. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids']
6. [spotify] For each playlist from step 5, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed playlist info including songs with their 'id'. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
7. [spotify] For each song in each playlist from step 6, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song['id']) to get the song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
8. [spotify] Remove from each playlist all songs with 'release_date' before '2021-01-01' using remove_song_from_playlist(playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id'], song_id=song['id'], access_token=token).

from datetime import datetime

token = login('spotify')
cutoff_date = datetime(2021, 1, 1)
songs_to_remove = []

for song in songs_with_release:
    release_str = song.get('release_date')
    if release_str:
        release_date = datetime.fromisoformat(release_str.split('T')[0])
        if release_date < cutoff_date:
            songs_to_remove.append(song['song_id'])

print(f"Songs to remove from library (released before 2021): {songs_to_remove}")

    call_api('spotify', 'remove_song_from_library', token, song_id=song_id)

from datetime import datetime

token = login('spotify')
cutoff_date = datetime(2021, 1, 1)
songs_to_remove_from_playlists = []

for entry in playlist_songs_release:
    release_str = entry.get('release_date')
    if release_str:
        release_date = datetime.fromisoformat(release_str.split('T')[0])
        if release_date < cutoff_date:
            songs_to_remove_from_playlists.append((entry['playlist_id'], entry['song_id']))

print(f"Songs to remove from playlists (released before 2021): {songs_to_remove_from_playlists}")

for playlist_id, song_id in songs_to_remove_from_playlists:

  - Ambiguities: The task specifies "released in or before 2021," so we interpret this as any song with release_date year <= 2021.
  - API quirks: 
    • show_song_library returns songs with 'song_id' and 'added_at' but no release date, so we must call show_song for each song to get 'release_date'.
    • show_playlist_library returns playlists with 'song_ids' array.
    • show_playlist returns detailed playlist info including 'songs' with 'id' (song_id).
    • Removal from library and playlist requires separate calls: remove_song_from_library and remove_song_from_playlist.

  3. [spotify] For EACH song from step 2, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song['song_id']) to get the song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
  4. [spotify] Filter songs from step 3 where the release year (from 'release_date') is less than or equal to 2021. [field: 'release_date']

  7. [spotify] For EACH song in each playlist from step 6, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', token, song_id=song['id']) to get the song details including 'release_date'. [field: 'release_date']
  8. [spotify] Filter songs from step 7 where the release year (from 'release_date') is less than or equal to 2021. [field: 'release_date']
  9. [spotify] For EACH song filtered in step 4, use call_api('spotify', 'remove_song_from_library', token, song_id=song['song_id']) to remove the song from the user's song library.
  10. [spotify] For EACH playlist and for EACH song filtered in step 8 that is in that playlist, use call_api('spotify', 'remove_song_from_playlist', token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id'], song_id=song['id']) to remove the song from the playlist.

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
# Parse and remember entities from both notes
import re

def parse_shares(content):
    shares = {}
    lines = content.splitlines()
    for line in lines:
        match = re.match(r'-\s*(.+?)\s*=>\s*\$(\d+)', line)
        if match:
            name = match.group(1).strip()
            amount = float(match.group(2))
            shares[name] = amount
    return shares

shares1 = parse_shares(notes_content[0]['content'])
shares2 = parse_shares(notes_content[1]['content'])

# Remember all shares
for name, amount in {**shares1, **shares2}.items():
    remember_entity(name, amount=amount)

token_phone = login('phone')
current_date_info = call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token_phone)
print(current_date_info)

ReAct step 8 / attempt 1
# Create a mapping from lowercase first name to email for contacts
name_to_email = {c['first_name'].lower(): c['email'] for c in contacts}

# Identify who hasn't paid yet and remember their email and amount
for entity in all_entities():
    name_lower = entity['name'].lower()
    if name_lower not in paid_names:
        email = name_to_email.get(name_lower)
        if email:
            remember_entity(entity['name'], email=email)
        else:
            print(f"Email not found for {entity['name']}")

# Print all entities with emails for verification
for e in all_entities():
    print(e)

ReAct step 9 / attempt 1
token_venmo = login('venmo')

for entity in all_entities():
    if 'email' in entity and entity['name'].lower() not in paid_names:
        amount = entity['amount']
        email = entity['email']
        response = call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token_venmo,
                            user_email=email, amount=amount, description='Work Dinner', private=False)
        print(f"Payment request sent to {entity['name']} ({email}): {response}")

# Complete the task after sending all requests
apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

1. [simple_note] Use login('simple_note') to get access token for simple_note.
2. [simple_note] Use fetch_all_pages('simple_note', 'search_notes', token, query='dinner') to find notes related to the dinner. [field: 'title']
3. [simple_note] For each note found, use call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', token, note_id=note['note_id']) to get the note content with individual shares. [field: 'content']
4. [phone] Use login('phone') to get access token for phone.
5. [phone] Use call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', login('phone')) to get today's date. Compute yesterday's date string in YYYY-MM-DD format.
6. [venmo] Use login('venmo') to get access token for venmo.
7. [venmo] Use fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token, min_created_at=yesterday_date, max_created_at=yesterday_date, direction='received') to get transactions received yesterday (friends who paid). [field: 'sender.email']
8. [phone] For each friend name extracted from the note who owes money, use fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', token, query=friend_name) to get their email. [field: 'email']
9. [venmo] For each friend who owes money and has an email, check if they are in the list of senders from step 7. If not, use call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token, user_email=friend_email, amount=owed_amount, description='Friends Dinner', private=False) to send a payment request.

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
import re

note_content = notes_content[1]['content']  # content of note_id 3084
lines = note_content.split('\n')
pattern = r'- (.+) => \$([0-9]+)'

for line in lines:
    match = re.match(pattern, line.strip())
    if match:
        name = match.group(1).strip()
        amount = int(match.group(2))
        remember_entity(name, amount=amount)

print(all_entities())

ReAct step 9 / attempt 1
token_venmo = login('venmo')
transactions = fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token_venmo,
                              min_created_at='2023-05-19',
                              max_created_at='2023-05-19',
                              direction='received')

paid_emails = {tx['sender']['email'] for tx in transactions}

for e in all_entities():
    email = e.get('email')
    amount = e.get('amount')
    if email and email not in paid_emails:
        call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token_venmo,
                 user_email=email, amount=amount, description='Friends Dinner', private=False)

apis.supervisor.complete_task(answer=None)

APP: simple_note, venmo, phone
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to find the note in simple_note where the individual shares for the dinner are recorded. Then we need to find all Venmo transactions from yesterday to identify who has already paid. We also need to find the contacts of coworkers from the phone app to get their emails for payment requests.
  - Metric: For simple_note, we use search_notes to find notes and show_note to get the content. For venmo, show_transactions with date filtering by created_at to find payments made yesterday. For phone, search_contacts to find coworkers by name or phone number.

  - API quirks: simple_note search_notes does not return content, so we must call show_note for content. Venmo show_transactions supports min_created_at and max_created_at for date filtering. Phone search_contacts supports query and returns email addresses.

PLAN:
1. [simple_note] Use login('simple_note') to get the access token for simple_note.
2. [venmo] Use login('venmo') to get the access token for venmo.
3. [phone] Use login('phone') to get the access token for phone.
4. [phone] Use call_api('phone', 'get_current_date_and_time', token) to get today's date; compute yesterday's date for filtering venmo transactions. [field: 'date']
5. [simple_note] Use fetch_all_pages('simple_note', 'search_notes', token, query='dinner') to find notes related to the dinner. [field: 'title']
6. [simple_note] For each note from step 5, use call_api('simple_note', 'show_note', token, note_id=note['note_id']) to get the note content. Parse the content to extract coworker names and their individual shares. [field: 'content']
7. [venmo] Use fetch_all_pages('venmo', 'show_transactions', token, min_created_at=yesterday, max_created_at=yesterday) to get all transactions from yesterday. [field: 'created_at']
8. [phone] For each coworker name from the note, use fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', token, query=name) to find their email addresses. [field: 'email']
9. [generic] Compare the coworker list from the note with the venmo transactions to identify who has already paid (by matching emails) and who has not.
10. [venmo] For each coworker who has not paid, use call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token, user_email=email, amount=share, description='Dinner with Colleagues', private=False) to send a payment request with the description note "Dinner with Colleagues". [field: 'user_email', 'amount', 'description']

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

import re

def parse_shares(content):
    shares = {}
    lines = content.splitlines()
    for line in lines:
        match = re.match(r'-\s*(.+?)\s*=>\s*\$(\d+)', line)
        if match:
            name = match.group(1).strip()
            amount = int(match.group(2))
            shares[name] = amount
    return shares

all_shares = {}
for note in notes_content:
    shares = parse_shares(note.get('content', ''))
    all_shares.update(shares)

for name, amount in all_shares.items():
    remember_entity(name, amount=amount)
print("Remembered entities with shares:", all_shares)

token_phone = login('phone')
all_entities_list = all_entities()
name_to_email = {}

for entity in all_entities_list:
    name = entity['name']
    contacts = fetch_all_pages('phone', 'search_contacts', token_phone, query=name)
    if contacts:
        email = contacts[0].get('email')
        if email:
            name_to_email[name] = email

print("Name to email mapping from phone contacts:")
print(name_to_email)

paid_emails = {t['sender_email'] for t in transactions}
print("Emails who have paid:", paid_emails)

# Mark entities with email and payment status
for entity in all_entities_list:
    email = name_to_email.get(entity['name'])
    entity['email'] = email
    entity['paid'] = email in paid_emails if email else False
    remember_entity(entity['name'], email=email, paid=entity['paid'])

print("Entities with payment status:")
for e in all_entities():
    print(e)

APP: file_system
REASONING:
- Scope: We will iterate all files in the "~/photographs/vacations/" directory to organize them by their creation dates into subdirectories named after the vacation spots.
- Metric: The creation date of each file is in the 'created_at' field from the show_file API response.
- Ambiguities: The task specifies that files created in March correspond to Rome, April to Santorini, and all others to Berlin. We interpret "March" and "April" as the month part of the 'created_at' timestamp.
- API quirks: The show_directory API returns a list of file paths as strings. To get the creation date, we must call show_file for each file. The move_file API requires source and destination file paths and supports an overwrite flag and retain_dates flag.

PLAN:

4. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_directory', token, directory_path='~/photographs/vacations/Rome', recursive=True) to create the Rome subdirectory if it does not exist.
5. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_directory', token, directory_path='~/photographs/vacations/Santorini', recursive=True) to create the Santorini subdirectory if it does not exist.
6. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_directory', token, directory_path='~/photographs/vacations/Berlin', recursive=True) to create the Berlin subdirectory if it does not exist.
7. [file_system] FOR EACH file from step 3: Determine the month from 'created_at'. If March, move the file to '~/photographs/vacations/Rome/<original_file_name>'; if April, move to '~/photographs/vacations/Santorini/<original_file_name>'; otherwise move to '~/photographs/vacations/Berlin/<original_file_name>'. Use call_api('file_system', 'move_file', token, source_file_path=file, destination_file_path=destination_path, overwrite=False, retain_dates=True) to move the file while retaining original dates. from datetime import datetime

token = login('file_system')
base_dir = '/home/spencer/photographs/vacations'

for file_info in file_details:
    path = file_info['path']
    created_at = file_info['created_at']
    month = datetime.fromisoformat(created_at).month
    filename = path.split('/')[-1]
    if month == 3:
        dest_dir = 'Rome'
    elif month == 4:
        dest_dir = 'Santorini'
    else:
        dest_dir = 'Berlin'
    dest_path = f"{base_dir}/{dest_dir}/{filename}"
    # Move the file, do not overwrite existing files, retain dates
    call_api('file_system', 'move_file', token,
             source_file_path=path, destination_file_path=dest_path,
             overwrite=False, retain_dates=True)
print("Files moved successfully.")

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

ReAct step 5 / attempt 1
from datetime import datetime

token = login('file_system')
base_dir = '/home/susan/photographs/vacations'
athens_dir = base_dir + '/Athens'
seoul_dir = base_dir + '/Seoul'
paris_dir = base_dir + '/Paris'

for file in file_details:
    path = file['path']
    created_at = file['created_at']
    month = datetime.fromisoformat(created_at).month if created_at else None
    filename = path.split('/')[-1]
    if month == 1:
        dest_dir = athens_dir
    elif month == 4:
        dest_dir = seoul_dir
    else:
        dest_dir = paris_dir
    dest_path = dest_dir + '/' + filename
    result = call_api('file_system', 'move_file', token,
                      source_file_path=path,
                      destination_file_path=dest_path,
                      overwrite=False,
                      retain_dates=True)
    print(f"Moved {path} to {dest_path}: {result.get('message')}")

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

import re

# Extract the total amount from the bill content using regex for a dollar amount
amount_match = re.search(r'Total Amount Due[:\s]*\$?(\d+(?:\.\d{2})?)', bill_content, re.IGNORECASE)
if not amount_match:
    # Try alternative pattern if the first fails
    amount_match = re.search(r'Amount[:\s]*\$?(\d+(?:\.\d{2})?)', bill_content, re.IGNORECASE)

if amount_match:
    total_amount = float(amount_match.group(1))
else:
    # If no amount found, assume a default or fail gracefully
    total_amount = None

print("Extracted total amount:", total_amount)

if total_amount is None:
    print("Could not find total amount in bill content. Cannot proceed with payment requests.")
else:
    roommates_count = len(all_entities())
    share_amount = round(total_amount / (roommates_count + 1), 2)  # +1 for Jose himself
    print(f"Each roommate should pay: ${share_amount}")

    for roommate in all_entities():
        email = roommate.get('email')
        if email:
            response = call_api('venmo', 'create_payment_request', token_venmo,
                                user_email=email,
                                amount=share_amount,
                                description='For electricity bill.',
                                private=False)
            print(f"Payment request sent to {email}: {response}")

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's song library, all albums in the user's album library (and their songs), and all playlists in the user's playlist library (and their songs). This ensures we get a unique list of all songs across these containers.
  - Metric: The song title is in the 'title' field and artists are in the 'artists' list with each artist having a 'name' field. These fields are confirmed by the show_song_library, show_album_library, show_playlist_library, show_playlist, and show_song APIs.
  - Ambiguities: The task requires a unique list of songs, so duplicates across libraries and playlists must be removed. Artists should be concatenated with "|" separator. The file path is "~/backups/spotify.csv" which is relative to the user's home directory.
  - API quirks: 
    * show_playlist returns songs with 'id', 'title', and 'artist_ids' but not full artist names, so we must get artist names from the song or album details.
    * show_album returns songs with 'id', 'title', and 'artist_ids' but full artist names are in the album's 'artists' list.
    * show_song_library and show_album_library provide full artist names directly.
    * We must login to both spotify and file_system apps.
    * Deleting the spotify account requires only the access_token.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the Spotify access token for all subsequent Spotify API calls.
2. [file_system] Use login('file_system') to get the File System access token for file operations.
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', spotify_token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'title', 'artists' (list of dicts with 'name')]
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', spotify_token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'songs' (list of songs with 'id', 'title', 'artist_ids'), 'artists' (list of dicts with 'name')]
5. [spotify] For each album from step 4, extract all songs with their titles and artist names (from album artists). Aggregate all album songs into a list.
6. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', spotify_token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id', 'song_ids']
7. [spotify] For each playlist from step 6, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', spotify_token, playlist_id=playlist_id) to get detailed playlist info including songs with 'id', 'title', and 'artist_ids'.
8. [spotify] For each song in playlists, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id) to get full song details including artist names.
9. [generic] Combine all songs from steps 3, 5, and 8 into one list. Remove duplicates by song title and artist names combination. Format artists as a string separated by "|".
10. [file_system] Create CSV content with headers "Title,Artists" and each song on a new line with title and artists string. Use call_api('file_system', 'create_file', file_system_token, file_path="~/backups/spotify.csv", content=csv_content, overwrite=True) to save the file.
11. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'delete_account', spotify_token) to terminate the Spotify account.

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

albums_library = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', spotify_token)

    album_artists = [artist['name'] for artist in album.get('artists', [])]
    artists_str = "|".join(album_artists)
    # We only have song_ids in album, so fetch each song's details to get title and artists

spotify_token = login('spotify')
playlist_songs = []

playlists_library = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', spotify_token)
for playlist in playlists_library:
    playlist_id = playlist.get('playlist_id')
    if playlist_id is not None:
        playlist_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', spotify_token, playlist_id=playlist_id)
        songs = playlist_detail.get('songs', [])
        for song in songs:
            song_id = song.get('id')
            if song_id is not None:
                full_song = call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id)
                artists_names = [artist.get('name', '') for artist in full_song.get('artists', [])]
                artists_str = "|".join(artists_names)
                playlist_songs.append({'title': full_song.get('title', ''), 'artists': artists_str})

print(f"Total detailed songs from playlists: {len(playlist_songs)}")

ReAct step 9 / attempt 1
spotify_token = login('spotify')

# Fetch song library songs
songs_library = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', spotify_token)
song_library_songs = [{'title': s.get('title', ''), 'artists': "|".join([a.get('name', '') for a in s.get('artists', [])])} for s in songs_library]

# Fetch album songs (reuse previous step 5 code)
albums_library = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', spotify_token)
album_songs = []
for album in albums_library:
    album_artists = [artist['name'] for artist in album.get('artists', [])]
    artists_str = "|".join(album_artists)
    for song_id in album.get('song_ids', []):
        song = call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id)
        album_songs.append({'title': song.get('title', ''), 'artists': artists_str})

# Fetch detailed playlist songs (reuse previous step 8 code)
playlists_library = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', spotify_token)
playlist_songs = []
for playlist in playlists_library:
    playlist_id = playlist.get('playlist_id')
    if playlist_id is not None:
        playlist_detail = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', spotify_token, playlist_id=playlist_id)
        songs = playlist_detail.get('songs', [])
        for song in songs:
            song_id = song.get('id')
            if song_id is not None:
                full_song = call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id)
                artists_names = [artist.get('name', '') for artist in full_song.get('artists', [])]
                artists_str = "|".join(artists_names)
                playlist_songs.append({'title': full_song.get('title', ''), 'artists': artists_str})

# Combine all songs
all_songs = song_library_songs + album_songs + playlist_songs
print(f"Total songs before deduplication: {len(all_songs)}")

# Deduplicate by (title, artists)
seen = set()
unique_songs = []
for song in all_songs:
    key = (song['title'], song['artists'])
    if key not in seen:
        seen.add(key)
        unique_songs.append(song)

print(f"Total unique songs after deduplication: {len(unique_songs)}")
if unique_songs:
    print("Sample unique song:", unique_songs[0])

ReAct step 10 / attempt 1
file_system_token = login('file_system')

# Prepare CSV content
lines = ["Title,Artists"]
for song in unique_songs:
    # Escape quotes and commas in title and artists if needed
    title = song['title'].replace('"', '""')
    artists = song['artists'].replace('"', '""')
    # Wrap fields in quotes to handle commas
    line = f'"{title}","{artists}"'
    lines.append(line)
csv_content = "\n".join(lines)

# Save to file
file_path = "~/backups/spotify.csv"
result = call_api('file_system', 'create_file', file_system_token, file_path=file_path, content=csv_content, overwrite=True)
print(f"File saved to {file_path}: {result}")

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

unique_songs = {}
for song in all_songs_details:
    sid = song.get('song_id')
    if sid not in unique_songs:
        title = song.get('title', '').replace('"', '""')
        artists = song.get('artists', [])
        artist_names = "|".join(artist.get('name', '').replace('"', '""') for artist in artists)
        unique_songs[sid] = f'"{title}","{artist_names}"'

formatted_songs = "\n".join(unique_songs.values())
print(f"Formatted {len(unique_songs)} unique songs for CSV.")
print("Sample CSV line:", list(unique_songs.values())[0])

📋 Explorer Plan
APP: spotify, file_system
REASONING:
  - Scope: We need to iterate all songs in the user's song library, all albums in the user's album library (and their songs), and all playlists in the user's playlist library (and their songs). This ensures we get a unique list of all songs across these containers.
  - Metric: The song title is in the 'title' field and artists are in the 'artists' list with each artist having a 'name' field. These fields are confirmed by the show_song_library, show_album_library, show_playlist, and show_song APIs.
  - Ambiguities: The task requires a unique list of songs, so duplicates across libraries and playlists must be removed. Artists should be concatenated with "|" separator.
  - API quirks: 
    - show_playlist returns songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids' but not full artist names, so we must get artist names from the song details.
    - show_album returns songs with 'id' and 'artist_ids' but not full artist names, so we must get artist names from the song details.
    - show_song provides full song details including 'title' and 'artists' with names.
    - The file_system create_file API requires an access_token and file_path starting with "~/", and content as a string.

PLAN:
1. [spotify] Use login('spotify') to get the access token for Spotify.
2. [file_system] Use login('file_system') to get the access token for File System.
3. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_song_library', spotify_token) to get all songs in the user's song library. [field: 'title', 'artists']
4. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_album_library', spotify_token) to get all albums in the user's album library. [field: 'album_id', 'song_ids']
5. [spotify] For each album from step 4, use call_api('spotify', 'show_album', spotify_token, album_id=album['album_id']) to get detailed album info including songs with their 'id'. Collect all song IDs from all albums. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
6. [spotify] Use fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', spotify_token) to get all playlists in the user's playlist library. [field: 'playlist_id']
7. [spotify] For each playlist from step 6, use call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', spotify_token, playlist_id=playlist['playlist_id']) to get detailed playlist info including songs with their 'id'. Collect all song IDs from all playlists. [field: 'songs' with 'id']
8. [generic] Combine all song IDs from steps 3 (song library), 5 (album songs), and 7 (playlist songs) into a unique set of song IDs.
9. [spotify] For each unique song ID from step 8, use call_api('spotify', 'show_song', spotify_token, song_id=song_id) to get song details including 'title' and 'artists' (with 'name').
10. [generic] Create CSV content string with headers "Title,Artists". For each song from step 9, add a line with the song's title and the artists' names joined by "|".
11. [file_system] Use call_api('file_system', 'create_file', file_system_token, file_path="~/backups/spotify_songs.csv", content=csv_content, overwrite=True) to create the CSV file with the collected song data.
12. [spotify] Use call_api('spotify', 'delete_account', spotify_token) to terminate the Spotify account.

csv_lines = ['Title,Artists']
for song in songs_details:
    title = song.get('title', '').replace('"', '""')
    artists = song.get('artists', [])
    artist_names = "|".join(artist.get('name', '').replace('"', '""') for artist in artists)
    # Quote fields if they contain commas or quotes
    if ',' in title or '"' in title:
        title = f'"{title}"'
    if ',' in artist_names or '"' in artist_names:
        artist_names = f'"{artist_names}"'
    csv_lines.append(f"{title},{artist_names}")

csv_content = "\n".join(csv_lines)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

print("Simple Note token:", token_simple_note)

import re

content = workout_note_full.get('content', '')
# Extract all durations in minutes from the note content using regex
durations = [int(x) for x in re.findall(r'duration_mins:\s*(\d+)', content)]

# Find the maximum duration for the workout session

print("Maximum workout duration (minutes):", max_duration)

avg_song_length = 3.5
min_songs_needed = int(max_duration / avg_song_length) + 1
print("Minimum number of songs needed for workout:", min_songs_needed)

ReAct step 1 / attempt 1
TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]
print("Login helper defined.")

ReAct step 4 / attempt 1
import re

content = exercise_plan_note.get('content', '')
# Extract duration for Monday as example (or we could parse all days)
monday_match = re.search(r'day: monday.*?duration_mins: (\d+)', content, re.DOTALL | re.IGNORECASE)
workout_duration = int(monday_match.group(1)) if monday_match else 30  # default 30 if not found

# Estimate number of songs needed assuming average 3 minutes per song
songs_needed = (workout_duration + 2) // 3  # round up

print(f"Workout duration (mins): {workout_duration}")
print(f"Estimated songs needed: {songs_needed}")

# Remember the songs needed for later steps
remember('songs_needed', songs_needed)

ReAct step 6 / attempt 1
token = login('spotify')
playlists = fetch_all_pages('spotify', 'show_playlist_library', token)

detailed_playlists = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_id = pl.get('playlist_id')
    if pl_id is not None:
        details = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token, playlist_id=pl_id)
        detailed_playlists.append({'playlist_id': pl_id, 'title': pl.get('title'), 'songs': details.get('songs', [])})

for dp in detailed_playlists:
    print(f"Playlist '{dp['title']}' has {len(dp['songs'])} songs.")

remember('detailed_playlists', detailed_playlists)

TOKENS = {}
def login(app):
    if app in TOKENS:
        return TOKENS[app]
    prof = apis.supervisor.show_profile()
    pw = next(c['password'] for c in apis.supervisor.show_account_passwords()
              if c['account_name'] == app)
    user = prof['phone_number'] if app == 'phone' else prof['email']
    TOKENS[app] = getattr(apis, app).login(username=user, password=pw)['access_token']
    return TOKENS[app]

import datetime
import re

# Extract the day of the week (lowercase)
today = datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%A').lower()

# Extract all day blocks with their duration_mins from the note content
content = workout_note.get('content', '')
day_blocks = re.findall(r'day: (\w+)(.*?)duration_mins: (\d+)', content, re.DOTALL)

# Find today's duration
duration_today = 0
for day, _, duration in day_blocks:
    if day == today:
        duration_today = int(duration)
        break

print(f"Today is {today}, workout duration in minutes:", duration_today)

# Now get Spotify token for next step
token_spotify = login('spotify')
print("Spotify access token:", token_spotify)

ReAct step 7 / attempt 1
playlist_details = []
for pl in playlists:
    pl_id = pl['playlist_id']
    details = call_api('spotify', 'show_playlist', token_spotify, playlist_id=pl_id)
    song_count = len(details.get('songs', []))
    playlist_details.append({'playlist_id': pl_id, 'title': pl.get('title'), 'song_count': song_count})

print("Playlist details with song counts:")
for pd in playlist_details:
    print(pd)

ReAct step 8 / attempt 1
# Filter playlists with enough songs
suitable_playlists = [pl for pl in playlist_details if pl['song_count'] >= duration_today]
print("Suitable playlists with enough songs:", suitable_playlists)

# If none suitable, pick the one with the most songs
if not suitable_playlists:
    max_songs = max(pl['song_count'] for pl in playlist_details)
    best_playlists = [pl for pl in playlist_details if pl['song_count'] == max_songs]
else:
    best_playlists = suitable_playlists

print("Best playlist(s) to play:", best_playlists)
